### Wave 84 — AI award-intake commit: natural-language dates no longer dropped

- AWARD-INTAKE-NLDATE-PARSE FIXED & MERGED (commit 188a4685 on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version a715423e). Follow-on to the Wave 82/83 award-intake hardening, found by tracing the commit date path against what Wave 83's live OpenRouter call actually returned. The model emits award dates in natural language (Wave 83 live call returned "July 1, 2026"/"June 30, 2027"), and the server-side canonicalizer (canonical-fields.ts) normalizes MONEY only — dates pass through verbatim. On the client commit path (extraction-review.tsx), `parseOptionalDateTime` only anchored bare ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` (appending `T00:00:00.000Z`); any other string — including every natural-language date — became `Invalid Date` and was SILENTLY OMITTED from the commit. Net: an accepted, 100%-confidence award period stated plainly in the document produced a grant with no start/end date. For compliance software ("cannot fail"), a lost award period is a real defect. FIX: rewrote `parseOptionalDateTime` to (1) anchor ISO date-only strings to UTC midnight as before; (2) preserve explicit ISO timestamps (contain a `T`/space + `HH:MM`) as their exact instant via `toISOString()`; (3) accept anything else `Date` can parse (natural language) and RE-ANCHOR to UTC midnight from the resolved calendar components so there is no timezone day-drift. Routed `displayDateInputValue` (placeholder formatter) through the same parser for a single source of truth. TDD: new test "commits natural-language dates the model emits instead of dropping them" (valueJson "July 1, 2026"/"June 30, 2027" → commit gets 2026-07-01/2027-06-30 UTC). RED confirmed (dates absent from the commit payload), then GREEN. An existing placeholder test initially broke because the first fix re-anchored full UTC timestamps via LOCAL getters (a real drift bug I introduced); corrected by branching on explicit-time strings — net the suite caught my own regression. Touched file 100% stmts/funcs/lines, 98.12% branch (>=95 gate). GATE: built web first (build-output.test.ts gotcha); web typecheck clean; full web suite 4014 pass; heavy pre-commit hook ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage green (exit 0, ~2m51s). REVIEW: self-review of the diff (sub-agents overflow the 200k context budget in this environment, so per CLAUDE.md Codex-runtime policy the review was performed locally) — confirmed the three-case branch is exhaustive for the field set, hoisting covers the forward reference, and the placeholder regression was the parser catching itself. Merged to master (ff), worktree removed + branch deleted, pushed, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: this is the third consecutive award-intake defect (82 data-loss, 83 model-pin, 84 date-parse) surfaced by following the LIVE extraction output rather than static inspection — the marquee AI path rewards end-to-end tracing of real model output against every downstream coercion.

### Wave 85 — AI award-intake edit: edited money fields canonicalized to cents

- AWARD-INTAKE-EDIT-MONEY-CANONICAL FIXED & MERGED (commit 9185c94d on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-api-production version adea7e3c). The Wave 84 candidate (amountCents survival through the client path) traced to a SERVER defect on the review edit action (service.ts `recordDocumentExtractionAction`). At extraction time money is canonicalized to integer cents (canonical-fields.ts, `canonicalizeExtractionField` over MONEY_FIELDS = amountCents/approvedAmountCents/allocatedAmountCents), but the EDIT action stored `normalizedValueJson: params.input.nextValue` — the reviewer's raw STRING — verbatim for every field. So if a reviewer edited the award amount at all (even to a correct cents value like "30000000", let alone "$50,000"), it was persisted as a string. The client commit guard accepts amounts only via `typeof extractedAmountCents === "number"`, so ANY edited amount was silently dropped from the committed grant — the exact data-loss class as Waves 82/84, on the edit path. (accept/reject/defer send nextValue undefined → Drizzle skips the column → model's canonicalized cents survive; only edit overwrote.) FIX: new `canonicalizeEditedValue(fieldRow, nextValue)` helper runs the edited value through the SAME `canonicalizeExtractionField` (captured the field row that `assertExtractionField` already returns — was being discarded) and stores `canonical.normalizedValue ?? nextValue`. Money coerces to cents ("$50,000" → 5000000); non-money fields and uncoercible input fall through unchanged; undefined (non-edit actions) → undefined → column skipped, behavior preserved. Generic across all three money fields (also fixes budget-line/allocation amount edits). The grant alias table already maps `amountcents → amountCents`, so the canonicalizer's early-return-on-unknown-field guard does not block coercion (verified). TDD: 2 new service.test.ts tests — edited "$50,000" → normalizedValueJson 5000000 (RED confirmed: was stored as the raw string), and edited non-money name → "Renamed Award" unchanged; the pre-existing `{normalizedValueJson:"Edited"}` test still passes (its mock field has no destination metadata → canonicalizer no-ops → string preserved), proving backward-safety. Touched file 100% stmts/funcs/lines, 95.7% branch (>=95 gate). GATE: api typecheck clean; heavy pre-commit hook ran full @grantpipe/api typecheck + test:coverage green (exit 0). REVIEW: self-review of the diff (sub-agents overflow the 200k context budget in this environment; per CLAUDE.md Codex-runtime policy, reviewed locally) — confirmed the helper is a pure pass-through for the existing extraction-time logic, the captured fieldRow is the same row already validated, and every non-edit action preserves prior behavior. Merged to master (ff), worktree removed + branch deleted, pushed, deployed grantpipe-api via Wrangler (first attempt hit a transient wrangler failure; retry deployed cleanly — award-intake queue consumer now canonicalizes edited money). SWEEP NOTE: fourth consecutive award-intake fix (82 commit data-loss, 83 model-pin, 84 client date-parse, 85 server edit-money). The pattern across all four: every value the model/user introduces must survive each downstream coercion boundary unchanged or be deliberately transformed — the bugs live at the boundaries (normalize-vs-raw, string-vs-number, ISO-vs-natural-language). Award-intake now consistently canonicalizes money at BOTH extraction and edit, and parses dates (ISO + natural-language) at commit. Next candidate: confirm the reporting_requirement / restriction_term / closeout_item object-field round-trip (edit stores a JSON string into an object slot) commits cleanly, or flag if the edit box's JSON contract has a similar coercion gap.

### Wave 86 — AI award-intake object-field round-trip: VERIFIED CLEAN (no fix; UX candidate flagged)

- AWARD-INTAKE-OBJECT-ROUNDTRIP REVIEWED, NO DEFECT. Traced the Wave 85 follow-up candidate (the reporting_requirement / restriction_term / closeout_item object-field edit round-trip) through the full server commit path (service.ts `commitDocumentExtraction`). FINDING: the data path is robust, not gapped. (1) The edit box stores an object field as a JSON string (FieldReviewControls → `displayValue` = JSON.stringify), but the commit's `jsonRecord` (service.ts:676) explicitly re-parses stringified-JSON objects (`trimmed.startsWith("{")` → `JSON.parse`) back into records before reading leaves — the comment at line 677 documents this as a known model-output case that was already handled. (2) Object leaves are read via `jsonString`/`jsonNumber`/`jsonDate`; `jsonDate` (line 670) uses bare `new Date(value)` with NaN-guarding, so it parses natural-language dates (e.g. a reporting dueDate of "January 31, 2027") without the T-appending bug Wave 84 fixed on the client. (3) `scalar = record === null ? value : null` (line 953) correctly prevents a whole JSON blob from being written into a scalar text column. (4) The grant's own name/amount/dates come from the client-computed `requiredGrantBasics` (service.ts:855-863), which Waves 84 (date parse) + 85 (edit-money canonicalization) now make sound end-to-end. CONCLUSION: every value the model or reviewer introduces survives each coercion boundary on the commit path — the "cannot fail / no silent data loss" bar is met for award-intake. No code change (would not fabricate a fix where the path is verified sound — directive: "don't trust previous sweeps", so this candidate was traced rather than assumed, and cleared). FLAGGED UX CANDIDATE (next wave, larger + standalone): the review edit box renders object-valued fields as RAW JSON in a single-line `<Input>` (`{"reportType":"Annual Report","dueDate":"2027-01-31"}`). The data round-trips fine, but hand-editing JSON fails the Design-Canon bar ("the consultant is the competitor; every UI must be self-evident" — an 80-year-old cannot edit a JSON literal). Proper fix = a structured per-sub-field editor for object fields (render reportType/dueDate as labeled inputs, reassemble to the object on save), which is a focused redesign deserving its own session/context rather than a rushed tail-of-context change on the marquee AI surface. SWEEP NOTE: this closes the award-intake commit-path data-integrity thread opened in Wave 82 — four boundary fixes (82/83/84/85) + one verified-clean trace (86). The remaining award-intake work is UX/taste (JSON edit box), not correctness.

### Wave 87 — Structured editor for object-valued award-intake fields (UX, shipped)

- AWARD-INTAKE-OBJECT-EDIT-UX FIXED. The review edit box (`FieldReviewControls` in apps/web/src/components/document-extractions/extraction-review.tsx) previously seeded a single-line `<Input>` with `displayValue(...)`, which `JSON.stringify`s object-valued extraction fields (reporting_requirement / restriction_term / closeout_item) — forcing reviewers to hand-edit a raw brace-delimited JSON literal. Failed the Design-Canon bar ("every UI must be self-evident; an 80-year-old can use it without getting stuck"). FIX: new `ObjectFieldEditor` renders one labeled, pre-filled `<Input>` per sub-field (label via `humanizeKey`, e.g. "Report type", "Due date"), and on Save reassembles a `Record<string, unknown>` passed as the edit `nextValue` — `coerceToOriginalType` re-casts each edited string back to the sub-value's original type (number/boolean/string) so untouched values never silently change type. The redundant read-only key/value `<dl>` in `FieldValue` is dropped for objects (returns null) since the editable inputs now ARE the representation — no double-render, honoring "earned space / no redundancy". Server side needs no change: Wave 85 `canonicalizeEditedValue` passes objects through (restriction_term not in MONEY_FIELDS), and Wave 86 confirmed commit-time `jsonRecord` accepts objects directly. TDD: added "edits object fields with labeled sub-field inputs instead of raw JSON" (asserts no `^edit grant.terms$` single-line input, labeled inputs pre-filled, Save reassembles full object preserving untouched sub-field); updated the prior object-render test to assert the editor inputs rather than the removed dl. 23 file tests pass; coverage 98.42% stmts / 95.97% branch (≥95% gate); typecheck + lint clean; full web suite green through pre-commit. Merged ff to master (22823563), worktree removed, deployed grantpipe-web (version f9229736-9da7-4210-a067-be71b4b037a2). This closes the award-intake UX thread flagged at Wave 86 — the marquee AI surface no longer exposes raw JSON to reviewers.

SWEEP NOTE: the breadth pattern holds — irreversible mutations (delete grant/allocation) MUST carry pending guards, and every cents→input pre-fill must format to two decimals. The cross-file mock-shape fragility is itself a finding: a partial mutation mock that "worked" only because the missing key was read lazily in an onClick now breaks the moment the key is read at render — the fuller mock is the more honest fixture. Third breadth wave, executing the cohesive label-association candidate the Wave 89 ledger entry scoped. Across three files every `<Label>` that headed a `<Select>` lacked an `htmlFor`, so clicking the label word did nothing — only the box itself was a hit target. Added one contract test per file asserting each label/control id appears as both`htmlFor="x"`and`id="x"`, plus a `not.toMatch(/<SelectTrigger>/)` guard on portal-access proving no bare trigger remains. 86 tests pass across the three specs (16 team + 22 portal + 48 events). (5) journal/index.tsx empty state — `TeachAndActEmptyState` dropped the user on a bare "Journal entries" heading with no `description`; reused the already-shipped, already-vetted journal-entry sentence from accounting/index.tsx ("Use journal entries to fix balances. Add one for anything outside donations or grants.") for copy consistency. TDD: 4 new tests (RED by construction — each asserts an accessible name / radiogroup / description that did not exist pre-edit). Unlike the Wave 90 settings specs (which mock `@grantpipe/ui` Select and derive the name from aria-label), these accounting specs use the REAL Radix Select, so the tests assert true DOM accessible names via `getByRole("combobox", { name: ... })` and `getByRole("radiogroup", { name: "Import format" })` — a stronger contract than source-grep. The existing journal/new fund test queries `getAllByRole("combobox", { name: /fund/i })`, which still matches the new per-line `Fund for line N` names (verified). 166 tests pass across the 4 specs. Next wave: that accounting double-fire/pending-guard cluster.

### Wave 92 — Accounting double-fire/pending-guard cluster (shipped)

- ACCOUNTING-DOUBLE-FIRE FIXED & MERGED (commit c30a161d on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version a67fcde1-9b6c-44c5-b4c4-7d5c0dec2867). Fifth breadth wave — the three pending-guard defects Wave 91's survey explicitly deferred, each a fast-double-click hazard where the button's disable was driven by a mutation's `isPending` flag that lagged the action. (1) accounting/index.tsx "Enable" — `handleEnable` runs a 3-step sequential await chain (enable → seed COA → seed opening balances); the button disabled on `seedMutation.isPending || enableMutation.isPending`, but those don't flip until the first `mutateAsync` settles, so a second click before the first await resolved re-fired the whole irreversible sequence. FIX: local `isSubmitting` boolean set synchronously at the top of `handleEnable` (with an early `if (isSubmitting) return` guard) and cleared in `finally`; button now `disabled={!confirmAcknowledged || isSubmitting}` and labels off `isSubmitting`. (2) recurring.tsx "Run Now" — disabled on the SHARED `runMutation.isPending`, which (a) lagged the click and (b) would cross-disable EVERY row's Run Now during any single run. FIX: per-row `runningTemplateId` state set before the await and cleared in `finally`; `disabled={runningTemplateId === template.id}` so only the running row disables. (3) recurring.tsx Delete confirm — the confirm button relied on `deleteMutation.isPending` and only closed the dialog AFTER the await resolved, leaving a window for a second confirming click. FIX: `setTemplateToDelete(null)` now fires synchronously at the top of `handleDelete` (before the await), closing the dialog and unmounting the button on first click. TDD: 3 new RED tests using deferred (never-resolving) mock promises to hold each action in-flight and assert the guard — Enable double-click fires `enableFn` exactly once + button disables; Run Now disables only row 0 (row 1 stays enabled) and re-enables after resolve; Delete confirm button unmounts on first click + `mutateFn` called once. Also rewrote the pre-existing "shows Enabling… text" test (it drove the label off `enableMutation.isPending`, which my change decouples) to drive `isSubmitting` via a real click + deferred enable. 100 tests pass across the 2 specs. GATE: built web first (build-output.test.ts gotcha); tsc clean; FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN (exit 0, 2m25s). REVIEW: self-review of the 2-source + 2-test diff locally (sub-agents overflow the 200k context budget here; per CLAUDE.md Codex-runtime policy). Merged ff to master, worktree removed + branch deleted, pushed, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: `mutation.isPending` is the wrong primitive for double-fire prevention on any handler that does work BEFORE its first `mutateAsync` (multi-step chains) or shares one mutation across repeated rows — the disable lags the click. The robust idiom is a synchronously-set local guard (`isSubmitting` for single actions, an id-valued `runningX` for per-row), or closing/unmounting the trigger immediately. Worth a future targeted grep for `disabled={...isPending}` on multi-step or list-row buttons elsewhere.

### Wave 93 — TagPicker create double-submit guard (shipped)

- TAGPICKER-DOUBLE-SUBMIT FIXED & MERGED (commit 5f76dcd4 on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version a22c630a-13be-4318-b138-d4e21bbde7d1). Acted on Wave 92's SWEEP NOTE — an inline grep across all 44 `disabled={...isPending}` button files in apps/web/src for the two failure modes (pre-await work; shared mutation across list rows). The one genuine remaining instance: components/donors/tag-picker.tsx "Add" button. Its loading state was driven solely by the parent prop `isCreatingTag={createTag.isPending}` (donors/$contactId.tsx), but the parent's `handleCreateTag` chains TWO sequential mutations — `createTag.mutateAsync` then `addTags.mutateAsync([createdTag.id])`. Once the first resolves, `createTag.isPending` flips back to false while the SECOND await is still in flight, and the input isn't cleared until both finish — so a click (or Enter-key press) in that window fired a duplicate `createTag`. Mode-1 gap (guard covers only the first of two sequential mutations). Severity: minor/recoverable (duplicate tag), but a real correctness defect for the time-poor "click again because nothing happened" user. FIX placed at the correct layer — the button that fires the action owns its own debounce: added a self-contained `isSubmitting` state in TagPicker, set synchronously at the top of `handleCreateTag` (`if (!trimmed || isSubmitting) return`), cleared in `finally`, covering the ENTIRE `await onCreateTag(...)` operation (both parent awaits) AND the Enter-key path, independent of whatever the parent's `isCreatingTag` prop reports. Button now `disabled={!newTagName.trim() || isCreatingTag || isSubmitting}`, label `{isCreatingTag || isSubmitting ? "Adding..." : "Add"}` (prop retained for backward-compat/immediate parent feedback). Chose component-local over patching the parent's prop to `createTag.isPending || addTags.isPending` because `addTags` is also used by the tag-TOGGLE path (line 433), so an OR there would spuriously cross-disable Add during an unrelated toggle. TDD: 1 new RED test (deferred never-resolving `onCreateTag` mock → click Add → assert "Adding…" disabled → second click does NOT re-fire → `onCreateTag` called exactly once → resolve → re-enables); tested in tag-picker.test.tsx (the parent's contactId test mocks TagPicker and drops the prop, so the component's own spec is the only place the guard is observable). 15 tests pass. GATE: built web first; tsc clean; FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN (exit 0, 2m29s). REVIEW: self-review of the 1-source + 1-test diff locally (per CLAUDE.md Codex-runtime policy). Merged ff to master, worktree removed + branch deleted, pushed, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: the 44-file grep otherwise came back clean for these two modes — remaining `disabled={...isPending}` buttons are single-mutation, non-listed, no pre-await work (isPending IS sufficient there). The reusable lesson: a debounce guard belongs on the component that OWNS the trigger, not threaded down as a prop from a parent whose mutation flags may not span the whole user-perceived operation.

### Wave 94 — Live OpenRouter award-intake e2e: budget-line + match extraction gap (shipped)

- AWARD-INTAKE-EXTRACTION FIXED & MERGED (commit f5c304bd on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-api version 678cf46d-36ce-437c-8d30-86a61083f402). First wave to execute the Stop-hook-mandated LIVE OpenRouter leg: actually calling the paid award-intake model with the local `apps/api/.dev.vars` secret against a real PDF, not just unit-mocking it. Built a throwaway probe (apps/site/scripts, NOT committed — it loads the secret path) that renders a realistic notice-of-award letter to PDF via puppeteer, then invokes the REAL `extractAwardDocumentWithOpenRouter` (google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite + file-parser plugin) and prints the structured extraction for human review. FIRST RUN (prompt v2) surfaced a genuine quality defect: the letter stated an itemized approved budget (Personnel $150k, Supplies $40k, Evaluation $25k, Indirect $35k) and a "minimum 1:1 cash match" — the model captured NEITHER. Field count 10, with zero budget_line and zero matching restriction. Root cause: AWARD_INTAKE_SYSTEM_PROMPT listed budget_line/allocation/matching as available destinations but never INSTRUCTED the model to emit one entry per budget category or to capture cost-share terms (it only had per-report and per-requirement rules). For a paid feature, silently dropping the approved-budget table and the match obligation is a sell-blocking correctness gap. FIX: two new prompt rules — "When the document itemizes an approved budget, emit one budget_line entry per category … each with its own approvedAmountCents. Do not collapse … or skip it." and "Capture any matching, cost-share, or in-kind requirement as a restriction_term with restrictionType matching …" — and bumped AWARD_INTAKE_PROMPT_VERSION v2→v3. RE-PROBED LIVE (prompt v3): field count rose 10→14 — all four budget lines extracted with correct cents (15000000/4000000/2500000/3500000, summing exactly to the $250k total award) and the match captured as a restriction_term of restrictionType "matching" with the 1:1 ratio in its purposeStatement. Verified the commit pipeline accepts it: restriction_type is a free `text` column (packages/db/src/schema/restrictions.ts:23), so "matching" flows straight through service.ts's restriction_term insert without enum breakage. TDD: 3 new RED tests in openrouter.test.ts (prompt asserts "one budget_line entry per", a /match/i instruction, and version === "award-intake-v3"); two failed pre-fix as expected. openrouter.ts holds 100% stmts/branch/funcs/lines. GATE: FOREGROUND gated commit ran @grantpipe/api typecheck + full test:coverage GREEN (exit 0, 16.8s). REVIEW: self-review of the 1-source + 1-test diff locally (per CLAUDE.md Codex-runtime policy). Merged ff to master, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-api via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: this is the first defect found by ACTUALLY CALLING OpenRouter rather than reasoning about the code — the unit tests all passed both before and after because they mock the provider; only a live probe against a document with a real budget table exposes a prompt-completeness gap. The repeatable probe pattern (puppeteer-render a fixture PDF → real extraction → eyeball field coverage) is the right tool for future award-intake prompt regressions; worth re-running whenever the model id or prompt changes. Next live-leg candidates: probe other document shapes (multi-year budget, subaward, no-cost-extension) and the full upload→queue→review→commit path through the running api+web dev servers.

### Wave 95 — Live multi-shape extraction verification + empty-state heading consistency (shipped)

- Two parts this wave. PART A (live e2e, no fix needed): extended the throwaway OpenRouter probe to three more document shapes beyond Wave 94's single award letter — a multi-year federal Notice of Award (cooperative agreement, 25% match, quarterly SF-425 + final report), a university subaward agreement (pass-through entity, >$10k equipment prior-approval term), and a no-cost extension modification (amount unchanged, end date extended). Ran all three LIVE through `extractAwardDocumentWithOpenRouter` (v3 prompt). Result: extraction is accurate and complete across all three — funder/grant/amountCents (cents correct in every case: 120000000, 18550000, 7500000), ISO dates, contacts, reporting requirements, and the match captured as restrictionType matching on the federal NoA. The two arguable omissions are defensible, not defects: quarterly SF-425s ("due 30 days after each quarter") have no concrete dueDate the strict schema can hold, and annual budget PERIODS are not budget CATEGORIES (the v3 budget_line rule targets itemized categories like Personnel/Supplies). No fix manufactured where the output is correct — the v3 prompt holds across four distinct shapes. Probe left uncommitted (loads the secret path). PART B (visual-consistency fix, shipped): a static taste audit of empty-state headings found four `variant="empty"` titles ending with a trailing period — accounting/index.tsx "No journal entries yet.", notifications.tsx "No notifications yet." + "No notification preferences saved yet.", calendar.tsx "No deadlines scheduled this month." — while the dominant convention for these short noun-phrase headings is NO trailing period (anchor: dashboard.tsx "No donor metrics yet"). Distinguished from the legitimately period-terminated strings, which are Alert titles ("Unable to load X.") and auth subtitles — full sentences where the period is correct and consistent; those were left untouched. Body descriptions (each empty state's own `description`/children sentence) also keep their periods. Only the four headings were normalized. TDD: updated the 8 test assertions across notifications/calendar/accounting specs to the no-period strings FIRST (RED — 4 failing tests, "Unable to find element with text: …"), then dropped the periods from the four source titles (GREEN — 79 pass across the 3 specs). GATE: built web first (build-output.test.ts gotcha); tsc clean; FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN (exit 0, 2m35s; per-file coverage held — pure string edits, no branches). REVIEW: self-review of the 3-source + 3-test diff locally (per CLAUDE.md Codex-runtime policy). Merged ff to master (commit 160061b7), worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web version ec3f3626-8e0d-4991-b040-024d29f111ec via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: heading punctuation is a real (if small) sell-facing taste axis — a stray period on a short empty-state heading reads as unpolished next to its clean siblings. The convention is now uniform: short noun-phrase headings carry no terminal period; full-sentence Alert/error titles and body copy do. The static title=-prop audit (no-period 107 vs period 85, with the 85 being almost entirely correct full-sentence Alerts) is a repeatable check. Next visual axes worth a static sweep: button label casing consistency, icon-size uniformity within a surface, and loading-state copy ("Loading X…" ellipsis style). The full seeded-session visual walkthrough still needs a disposable E2E login (none in .env currently; signing up against prod is an outward-facing action to confirm before taking).

### Wave 96 — Pending-button ellipsis style consistency (shipped)

- ELLIPSIS-CONSISTENCY FIXED & MERGED (commit dfa56f5e on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version 2ba122e6-c661-4436-8e80-cf6c9add2107). Acted on Wave 95's flagged "loading-state copy ellipsis style" axis, and grounded it in the live seeded session: with api 5050 + web 3051 running and the seeded Dana Whitfield login, walked the dashboard, donors list, and the Add-donor modal — all read clean (intentional empty states, pill buttons, no-period headings, two-step progress wizard, correctly-disabled Next, 0 console errors). The concrete defect was static: pending/loading button labels mixed two ellipsis glyphs. A grep across apps/web/src for `(Loading|Saving|Creating|Exporting|…)…"` vs `..."` found 27 source files using ASCII "..." and only 3 files (4 strings) using the Unicode ellipsis (…): settings.portal-access.tsx "Exporting…", payments/index.tsx "Creating…", and programs/$programId.tsx ×2 "Saving…". Four clear outliers against an overwhelming ASCII majority — a small but real visual-consistency defect (two glyphs for the same loading idiom reads as unpolished in a sell-facing app). FIX: normalized the four to ASCII "..." to match the dominant convention (minimal, lowest-risk direction — make the minority match the majority rather than re-touch 77 strings). Left packages/ui/src/site/_ marketing components alone — they are a separate surface (email-capture/pricebook/fake-door) and internally consistent on "…". TDD: flipped the one affected assertion (payments/index.test.tsx:886 `name: "Creating…"` → `"Creating..."`) FIRST, ran it RED (getByRole button not found, source still emitted "Creating…"), then changed the four source strings → GREEN (123 pass across payments/programs/settings-portal-access specs). GATE: built web first (build-output.test.ts gotcha — first foreground commit attempt failed ONLY on that test because dist was stale; rebuilt, re-committed); FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN (exit 0, 2m36s; per-file coverage held — pure string edits, no new branches). REVIEW: self-review of the 4-source + 1-test diff locally (sub-agents overflow the 200k context budget here; per CLAUDE.md Codex-runtime policy). Merged ff to master, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: the apps/web pending-label convention is now uniformly ASCII "..."; the site (packages/ui/src/site) keeps its own "…" — two surfaces, each internally consistent, which is the defensible end state. The grep (`"(Loading|Saving|Adding|Deleting|Creating|Updating|Submitting|Sending|Uploading|Processing|Generating|Removing|Importing|Exporting)[^"]_…"` scoped to apps/web/src) is a repeatable regression check. Live seeded-session walkthrough is now unblocked (api 5050 + web 3051, Dana Whitfield) — dashboard/donors/add-donor surfaces verified clean this wave; next surfaces to walk: grants, funders, events, calendar, funds, programs, subrecipients, reports, cash/payments, accounting, settings, plus the full upload→queue→review→commit award-intake path through the running dev servers.

### Wave 97 — Session-error screen no longer dead-ends on a transient server error (shipped)

- SESSION-RETRY FIXED & MERGED (commit 91e52fd2 on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version 4877d9a0-9e82-4332-a03f-c9fdc1e21108). Found LIVE during the seeded-session walkthrough: navigating to /app/grants once surfaced a `500` on `GET /api/auth/better/get-session`, which flipped `useSession().error` and rendered the full-screen "Session expired" card — whose ONLY action was "Sign in" (→ /login). Reloading recovered cleanly (API log showed get-session back to 200), confirming the 500 was a transient blip (local: a wrangler worker reload; prod-analogue: Worker cold start or a brief Neon disconnect). The defect: better-auth's `useSession` does not auto-retry, and a `500` is a SERVER error, not an expired session — yet the screen treated ANY session-fetch error as terminal and booted an authenticated paying user to login on a recoverable hiccup. That is exactly the "it cannot fail" bar the goal calls out. FIX (minimal, robust, no error-classification guesswork): (1) use-session.ts now destructures better-auth's `refetch` and returns it as `refetchSession`. (2) \_authenticated.tsx's sessionError block gained a primary "Try again" button (`void refetchSession?.()`, optional-chained for mock/forward safety) that re-runs the session fetch in place; "Sign in" stays as the secondary outline action for genuine expiry. On a transient error, Try again refetches → 200 → app renders, no logout. On true expiry, Try again re-fails harmlessly and the user clicks Sign in. The two-button flex-row layout (`flex flex-col gap-3 sm:flex-row sm:justify-center`) mirrors the existing paywall/blocked-state screens in the same file (lines ~261-272), so it's visually consistent by construction. Existing copy left verbatim (no marketing-pass trigger); the only new UI string is the action verb "Try again", consistent with the file's existing "Retry" affordance on the contextError screen. TDD: 2 new RED tests — use-session.test.ts asserts the hook returns `refetchSession === ` better-auth's refetch fn; \_authenticated.test.tsx asserts the session-error screen renders a "Try again" button that calls refetchSession exactly once and does NOT navigate to /login. Both failed pre-impl (no such return field / no such button), green post-impl (60 pass across the 2 specs). The two pre-existing session-error tests (assert "Session expired" + "Sign in", and Sign-in→login navigation) still pass unchanged — both elements remain. TSC gotcha: the better-auth `useSession` mock is typed to `BetterFetchError`, so a plain `new Error()` in the new hook test failed tsc (TS2739, missing status/statusText/error); switched it to `error: null` since the test asserts only refetchSession identity, not error-ness. GATE: built web first (build-output.test.ts gotcha); tsc clean; FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN (exit 0, 2m26s; per-file coverage held). REVIEW: self-review of the 2-source + 2-test diff locally (per CLAUDE.md Codex-runtime policy). Merged ff to master, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: this is the second live-session find (after Wave 94's OpenRouter probe) that static review would have missed — only actually driving the running app surfaced the transient 500 and the dead-end it triggers. General lesson for the rest of the sweep: any full-screen error/terminal state should offer a recovery affordance (refetch/retry), not just a destructive exit (sign out / login), unless the error is provably terminal. Worth auditing the other terminal screens (contextError already has Retry ✓; paywall is correctly terminal; the non-admin "workspace still being set up" screen only offers Sign out, which is appropriate since the user genuinely can't proceed). Walkthrough surfaces verified clean so far: dashboard, donors (list + Add-donor modal), grants (renders after retry). Next: funders, events, calendar, funds, programs, subrecipients, reports, cash, accounting, settings, and the award-intake upload→queue→review→commit path.

### Wave 98 — Funder Type select misreported and silently overwrote the stored type (shipped)

- FUNDER-TYPE DATA-INTEGRITY BUG FIXED & MERGED (commit 61797076 on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version 83ccb830-7169-4c6e-9a30-3b17f9b72ce7). Found LIVE during the seeded-session walkthrough of the Funders surface: the funder "The Hartwell Family Foundation" showed type badge "Other" on BOTH the list card and the detail-page header, but the editable **Type dropdown** on the detail Overview tab showed "Foundation". Same record, two different types depending on which control you read. ROOT CAUSE: `$funderId.tsx` initialized the Type select's state with a lazy `useState(() => funderQuery.data?.type ?? "foundation")`. The component first renders during the query's loading phase (the loading guard at line ~127 returns before the form), so the lazy initializer runs while `funderQuery.data` is still `undefined` and pins `funderType` to the `"foundation"` fallback. The lazy initializer only runs once, so when the funder actually resolves, the state never updates — the select stays stuck on "foundation" for ANY funder whose real type isn't foundation. The name/website/priorities/notes inputs read `funder.*` directly each render and were correct; only the Type select (bound to the stale separate state) was wrong. SEVERITY: this isn't cosmetic — clicking "Save changes" submits `funderType`, so an Editor/Admin who opens a corporate/government/other funder and saves ANY edit (or just clicks Save) silently overwrites the stored type with "foundation". Silent server-side data corruption of a compliance record = exactly the "it cannot fail / cannot look bad to a big client" bar the goal sets. FIX: sync the select to the loaded type via the React "adjust state during render" pattern — track `lastSyncedType`, and when `funder.type` differs from it, set both `lastSyncedType` and `funderType`. This re-syncs whenever the loaded value changes (initial load, refetch-after-save) WITHOUT clobbering in-progress user edits (the loaded value is stable while the user is editing, so no reset fires). Chose adjust-during-render over a `useEffect` deliberately: the repo's ESLint `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rule rejects `setState` inside an effect (it flagged my first attempt), and the render-time pattern avoids the extra cascading commit. TDD: 1 new RED test in `$funderId.test.tsx` that — unlike the pre-existing Type tests, which mock `useFunder` as already-resolved on the first render and thus accidentally mask the bug — reproduces the real async sequence: render once with `{ data: undefined, isLoading: true }`, then rerender with `{ data: { ...baseFunder, type: "government" } }`, and assert BOTH that the Type combobox value is "government" AND that a blind Save submits `type: "government"` (not the stale "foundation"). RED: `expected 'foundation' to be 'government'`. GREEN after the render-time sync; all 57 tests in the file pass, and the two pre-existing save-type tests still pass (the sync doesn't fire mid-edit). GATE: rebuilt web first (build-output.test.ts dist gotcha); FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN (4m12s, per-file coverage held, $funderId.tsx ≥95%). REVIEW: self-review of the 13-line source diff + 37-line test locally (Codex-runtime policy). Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed via Wrangler. LIVE RE-VERIFICATION: reloaded the funder detail in the running seeded session post-deploy — Type dropdown now correctly reads "Other", matching the header and list badges; 0 console errors.
- LOCAL-ENV NOTE (not a product bug, recorded so the next session doesn't re-chase it): while verifying, the app repeatedly showed the Wave 97 "Session expired" screen with persistent `500`s on `/api/auth/better/get-session`. Diagnosis: these were NEVER product errors. (1) The local `wrangler dev` worker on 5050 must be launched via the harness's NATIVE `run_in_background` (not `nohup … & disown`, not `exec`, not a `tail -f /dev/null |` stdin-keepalive) — the detached variants exit cleanly within ~15s (wrangler's interactive stdin handling), which drops the API and surfaces as proxy 500s. Native background stays up indefinitely. (2) After restarting the worker, the Vite dev server's proxy must ALSO be relaunched with `GRANTPIPE_API_PORT=5050` in its env — without it, `getLocalApiOrigin()` falls back to the default `:8787`, so Vite proxies `/api/*` to a port with no listener and every call ECONNREFUSEs → 500. The "transient" symptom was entirely my own misconfigured restarts, not the get-session route. PROCESS-SAFETY CATCH: a broad `pkill -f workerd` I ran would have killed the SIBLING phiguard project's workerd (it runs on :3300) — do NOT pattern-kill workerd; target the exact grantpipe PID only. Wave 97's defensive "Try again" affordance still stands on its own merits (a real transient get-session 500 can still occur in prod on a Worker cold start), and it was observed rendering correctly live this session — but the specific 500 storm here was environmental, so no further get-session backend work is warranted. Funders surface (list + detail Overview) otherwise verified visually clean: consistent pills, warm palette, correct hierarchy. Next walkthrough targets: events, calendar, funds, programs, subrecipients, reports, cash, accounting, settings, and the award-intake upload→queue→review→commit path.

### Wave 99 — Dangling "Threshold --" spend-down badge on zero-allocation funds/grants (shipped)

- THRESHOLD-BADGE POLISH FIXED & MERGED (commit a61ceb90 on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version e191bf80-e5f0-4d8c-b487-5338b7b9e007). Found LIVE during the seeded-session walkthrough of the Funds surface: created a "Smith Family Scholarship" fund (Permanently restricted) and its detail-page header rendered a badge reading **"Threshold --"** — a label with a dangling em-dash placeholder. ROOT CAUSE: both `funds/$fundId.tsx` (line 172) and `grants/$grantId.tsx` (line 1484) rendered `<Badge>{formatThresholdLabel(summary.thresholdState)}</Badge>` UNCONDITIONALLY. `thresholdState` is the spend-down progress state ("80"/"90"/"100"), set by the API only when there is a budget/allocation to track (grants/summary.ts: `budget != null && budget > 0 ? deriveThresholdState(...) : null`); for a zero-allocation fund/grant it is `null`, and `formatThresholdLabel(null)` returns the literal string "Threshold --". So every freshly-created (un-allocated) fund or grant showed a broken-looking "Threshold --" chip in its header. The grant spend-down PANEL already guarded this correctly (`spendDown.thresholdState && (...)` at $grantId.tsx:2851) — the two HEADERS did not. NOT a format-function bug: `formatThresholdLabel`'s "Threshold --" contract is intentional and reused by the compliance CSV export (compliance/service.ts:1545), so it was left untouched; the fix is purely at the render site. FIX: wrap both header badges in `summary.thresholdState ? (<Badge…/>) : null` — omit the badge entirely when there is no spend-down to report, matching the existing panel guard. TDD: 2 new RED tests in `$fundId.test.tsx`(sparse fund with no summary asserts`queryByText(/^Threshold/)`absent — RED, badge rendered "Threshold --"; baseFund with thresholdState "75" asserts "Threshold 75%" present — passed), plus assertions added to 2 existing`$grantId.test.tsx`tests (stale-data test w/ thresholdState "80" asserts "Threshold 80%" present; Budget-tab test w/ null asserts no "Threshold" badge). GATE CAUGHT A REGRESSION: the first foreground gated commit FAILED — 3 pre-existing tests in`**tests**/grants-funds-funders-pages.test.tsx`(lines 1169/2662/2679) asserted`getByText("Threshold --")`present, encoding the OLD buggy behavior; all 3 were null-threshold scenarios, so updated them to`queryByText(/^Threshold/)`absent. (Confirmed the separate`getAllByText("--")`assertions in those tests still hold — those "--" come from`formatCurrencyCents(null)` on the balance cards, not the threshold badge.) Re-ran gate: FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN (2m53s, per-file coverage held — pure conditional + test edits). REVIEW: self-review of the 2-source + 3-test diff locally (Codex-runtime policy). Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed via Wrangler. LIVE RE-VERIFICATION: reloaded the Smith Family Scholarship fund detail in the running seeded session post-deploy — the "Threshold --" badge is gone; header now shows only the "Permanently restricted" type badge + Share; 0 console errors. ENV NOTE: the worker (native-bg task bx7sn3rft) had died by verification time and surfaced the Wave-98-documented false-alarm "Session expired" screen; relaunched via native run_in_background (task b6g38zhtt), worker up in 1s, seeded session intact. SWEEP NOTE: third live-only find — static review passes because the format function "works"; only rendering a real zero-allocation entity exposes the dangling-placeholder chip. General lesson: a "--"/em-dash placeholder is acceptable in a DATA CELL (a table column that's structurally always present) but reads as broken in a standalone BADGE/chip — prefer omitting the badge over rendering an empty-valued one. Funds surface (list empty state, create dialog, detail Overview + edit form — Type select correctly reflects stored type, no Wave-98-class bug here) otherwise verified clean. Next walkthrough targets: programs, subrecipients, reports, cash, accounting, settings, and the award-intake upload→queue→review→commit path.

### Wave 100 — Award-intake review screen exposed raw integer cents in every editable money field (shipped)

- INTAKE DOLLARS-VS-CENTS FIXED & MERGED (commit c7b463c6 on master, ff). Found LIVE while driving the full award-intake path through the running dev servers (api 5050 + web 3051, seeded Dana Whitfield trial org which HAS award-intake) against a real OpenRouter extraction: uploaded a synthetic Hartwell Family Foundation award letter ($325,000), and on the `/award-intake/$extractionId`review screen every EDITABLE money affordance showed the raw integer cents — the per-field amount editor pre-filled`32500000`, the sidebar manual amount input placeholdered `32500000`/"Award amount cents". A reviewer thinks in dollars; surfacing wire-format cents in an editable box is both a taste failure and a real data-entry hazard (type "400000" meaning $400k, save 4,000 dollars). The read-only `FieldValue`was already correct ($325,000.00 via Intl currency) — only the editable controls leaked cents. ROOT CAUSE:`extraction-review.tsx`used a single`parseOptionalAmountCents`helper that treated the input string as cents both ways, and the per-field scalar editor / sidebar input rendered the stored cents number verbatim. FIX: the wire/storage format stays integer cents (money invariant preserved); only the editable VIEW converts. Added`formatCentsAsDollarsInput` (cents→`(n/100).toFixed(2)`) and `parseDollarsToCents` (dollar string→`Math.round(n\*100)`, rejecting non-positive/non-finite). `FieldReviewControls`special-cases`destinationField === "amountCents"`: pre-fills the editor with the dollar string, renders a `$`-adorned `inputMode="decimal"` input (`aria-label="Edit award amount in dollars"`), disables Save when the dollar value isn't a positive number, and converts back to cents on save. The sidebar manual input got the same `$`-adornment + `aria-label="Award amount"`+ plain dollar placeholder;`resolvedAmountCents`now parses dollars; state renamed`grantAmountCents`→`grantAmountDollars`. Read-only `FieldValue`left untouched (already correct). TDD: updated the existing tests that encoded the old cents contract (type`123.45`→`amountCents: 12345`; `-100` rejected; placeholder "50.00"/"Award amount") and added 3 RED-first tests — edit dollars and save back as cents (`325000.00`→ type`400000.50`→`40000050`), Save blocked for non-positive/non-numeric dollar values (`0`, `abc`), plain dollar placeholder when nothing extracted. GATE: built web first (build-output.test.ts dist gotcha — first commit attempt failed ONLY on that; rebuilt, re-committed c7b463c6); FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN; per-file coverage held (extraction-review.tsx 98.51%, test 96.25%). REVIEW: sub-agent review of the source+test diff clean. Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. LIVE RE-VERIFICATION: reloaded the persisted extraction on the running dev server (HMR) — per-field amount editor now reads `325000.00`(was`32500000`), sidebar shows `$`adornment + "Award amount", read-only card still`$325,000.00`; 0 console errors. CLEANUP: removed a leftover uncommitted `apps/api/q.mjs`DB probe (loaded the secret path) before this ledger commit. SWEEP NOTE: fourth live-only find — static review passes because cents IS the correct wire value; only actually reviewing an extraction in the browser exposes that an editable box should speak dollars. General lesson: the money-as-cents invariant is a STORAGE/WIRE contract, not a DISPLAY one — every editable money input must convert to dollars on the way in and back to cents on the way out, exactly as read-only formatters already do. Repeatable check: grep editable money inputs for direct`amountCents`/cents-number binding without a `/100` view conversion. Next: exercise the "Commit reviewed setup" action end-to-end (accept/edit fields, create_new decisions, commit → grant record created), then continue auditing programs, subrecipients, reports, cash, accounting, settings.

### Wave 101 — Reports grant-compliance picker couldn't distinguish same-named grants (shipped)

- REPORT-PICKER DISAMBIGUATION FIXED & MERGED (commit f51649ba on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version e3eb072d-524e-4e82-8021-9450fc4d481d). Found LIVE while walking Reports → Grant compliance on the running dev servers after completing the Wave-100 intake commit path end-to-end (real OpenRouter extraction → accept 8 fields → create-new funder+grant → commit → "Mobile Dental Outreach Expansion" $325,000 grant created, dollars round-trip to cents through the real API; verified the full commit action, closing the Wave-100 "next" item). The grant compliance picker (`reports/index.tsx`) rendered each option as the bare `{grant.name}` only. Annual renewals are a common nonprofit pattern — two grants legitimately share a name (e.g. the same program funded in 2025 and 2026, or by two funders). With only the name showing, a reviewer cannot tell which grant they're generating a compliance packet for; selecting the wrong one produces a wrong-but-plausible packet. For "it cannot fail" compliance software that's a real hazard, not just a taste gap. The wire already carried the disambiguators: `grant.service.ts` list `.select` returns `funderName: funders.name`, `startDate`, `endDate` — only the client type + render dropped them. FIX: widened `ReportGrantOption` in `use-reports.ts` with optional `funderName?: string | null` and `startDate?: string | null` (runtime already preserved them via `grantOptions.push(...pageItems)`, so no branch change → coverage untouched); the SelectItem now renders a two-line option — grant name on top, a muted `funderName · periodYear` disambiguator below (period year = `startDate.slice(0,4)`), omitted entirely when neither field is present so single-grant orgs see no change. TDD: added a RED-first test (`disambiguates same-named grants in the picker by funder and period year`) with two same-named "Mobile Dental Outreach Expansion" options differing only by funder (Hartwell 2026 / Riverbend 2025), asserting both render with their disambiguators AND that selecting Riverbend calls the generate mutation with `grant-renewal-2025`; the existing default mock options carry no funderName/startDate so all prior exact-name option matches stay green. GATE: built web first (build-output.test dist gotcha); FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN, per-file coverage held. WORKTREE NOTE: recurring mistake again — initial edits/RED-GREEN landed in MAIN not the `.worktrees/report-grant-picker` worktree; corrected by patching the worktree (`git diff > patch`, `git apply`, verified) and reverting main, then re-ran typecheck/tests/build IN the worktree before the gated commit. REVIEW: diff reviewed clean (minimal, type+render only). Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: fifth live-only / data-bound find — the defect is invisible in an empty or single-grant state; only a list with genuine name collisions exposes it. General lesson: any picker that selects a record by id but displays a single human label must surface enough secondary identity (funder, period, owner) to disambiguate legitimate duplicates — names are not unique. Repeatable check: grep SelectItem/option renders that show one entity field while the system permits same-named records. Next: continue the sweep on cash/payments, settings, events, calendar, donors detail, import, notifications, activity, dashboard — focusing on interactive/data-bound states where these finds keep surfacing.

### Wave 102 — Payments grant picker collision + activity-log empty state had no description (shipped)

- TWO DATA-BOUND FIXES MERGED (commit 26e34270 on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version e13c64c9-4490-4454-ae20-2e443b615484). A sonnet Explore audit of cash/payments, settings, import, notifications, and activity surfaces returned settings/import/notifications CLEAN and surfaced two defects, both verified by hand against source before acting. (1) PAYMENTS PICKER COLLISION — `payments/index.tsx` `NewPaymentRequestDialog` rendered each grant `<SelectItem>` as bare `{grant.name ?? grant.id}`. Same as the Wave-101 reports defect: annual-renewal grants legitimately share a name, so a payment request (drawdown/reimbursement/invoice) could be filed against the wrong grant — a real money-routing hazard. `useGrants` already returns `funderName`/`startDate` on the wire (same grants list endpoint). FIX mirrors Wave 101 exactly: widened the local `GrantOption` type with optional `funderName?`/`startDate?`, render a two-line option (name + muted `funderName · periodYear`, period year = `startDate.slice(0,4)`, omitted when neither present via `filter(Boolean)`). (2) ACTIVITY EMPTY STATE — `activity.tsx` `TeachAndActEmptyState` passed only `heading="Activity log"` + a nav button, no `description`, even though an existing test was misleadingly named "...with heading and description". The component already supports an optional `description` (renders a `<p>` when present). Added a plain-language description ("Every change your team makes shows up here — who changed what, and when. Add a record to see your first entry.") so first-time users understand what will appear. TDD: added a RED assertion to the existing activity empty-state test for the description text; added a RED-first payments test mocking two same-named "Mobile Dental Outreach" grants (Hartwell 2026 / Riverbend 2025) asserting both disambiguators render. Both confirmed RED (2 failed / 79 passed), then GREEN (81 passed) after implementation. GATE: typecheck clean, built web first (build-output dist gotcha), FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN, per-file coverage held. WORKTREE: `.worktrees/picker-emptystate-polish` — this time edits/RED-GREEN landed correctly IN the worktree from the start (no main-vs-worktree slip). REVIEW: code-simplifier review of the diff clean (null/undefined guarded by filter(Boolean); consistent with reports pattern; one harmless unnecessary `/s` regex flag noted, not a bug). Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: sixth/seventh finds, both data-bound/empty-state — the picker-collision pattern has now recurred twice (reports + payments); the general lesson from Wave 101 holds: any picker selecting a record by id while displaying a single human label needs secondary identity. Worth a future grep sweep for remaining SelectItem renders that show one entity field where same-named records are possible (grants, funders, funds, programs). Next: continue the sweep on events, calendar, donors detail, dashboard, plus deeper data-bound flows.

### Wave 103 — Journal-entry fiscal-period pickers showed only the period name (shipped)

- FISCAL-PERIOD DATE-RANGE DISAMBIGUATION MERGED (commit e61fec1a on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version 680384b8-ed78-41eb-bbe7-81f93af2616c). Follow-on from a systematic sonnet grep sweep (prompted by the picker-collision pattern recurring in Waves 101+102) that catalogued every single-label `<SelectItem>` over collision-prone records in apps/web. Twelve were found; I triaged by whether the available secondary field is a STRONG disambiguator and the consequence is high — deliberately SKIPPING the funder/fund pickers whose only secondary field is `type` (foundation/corporate/government, or restriction class), because `type` does NOT distinguish two same-named records (two "Smith Foundation" funders both read "foundation") and the subtitle would be decorative noise (violates the earned-space principle). The strong, high-consequence case shipped here: both journal-entry fiscal-period pickers — `components/dialogs/new-journal-entry-dialog.tsx` (line ~192) and `routes/_authenticated/accounting/journal/new.tsx` (line ~202) — rendered only `{p.name}`. Period names like "FY2026 Q1" recur across years and have NO unique-name constraint; posting a journal entry to the wrong period is a real GL error and the period's start–end dates are the authoritative truth. FIX: both render a two-line option (name + muted `formatUtcCalendarDate(startDate) – formatUtcCalendarDate(endDate)`); `FiscalPeriodRow` already types `startDate`/`endDate` (no cast widening needed); reused the existing shared `formatUtcCalendarDate` already used on the periods-management page for consistency. TDD: added `startDate`/`endDate` to both test files' period mocks and a RED-first test in each asserting the date range renders ("Jan 1, 2026 … Mar 31, 2026"); both confirmed RED (2 failed / 67 passed) then GREEN (69 passed). The existing option-by-name matchers (`getByText("FY2026 Q1")`, the empty "No open periods" option) stay green because the name lives in its own span. GATE: typecheck clean, built web first (build-output gotcha), FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN, per-file coverage held. REVIEW: diff self-reviewed (minimal, mirrors the reports/payments pattern; both sites identical). Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. REMAINING from the grep sweep (queued, strong-disambiguator only): program pickers showing `{program.name}` where `code` is a unique secondary field (grants/$grantId.tsx ×2, programs/$programId.tsx, programs/index.tsx) and the event picker in donors/$contactId.tsx where `date` disambiguates recurring annual events. Funder/fund `type` pickers intentionally left as-is (weak disambiguator). Next: program-code pickers, then the event-date picker.

### Wave 104 — Program-code + event-date picker disambiguation (shipped)

- TWO STRONG-DISAMBIGUATOR PICKERS MERGED (commit 3b6c3624 on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version af0e41d1-7f2f-4788-879f-414ed76dacac). Closes the queued strong-disambiguator items from the Wave-103 grep sweep, completing the picker-collision family. (1) PROGRAM PICKERS — both program allocation `<SelectItem>` renders in `grants/$grantId.tsx` (the grant-level `GrantProgramAllocationsEditor` ~line 610 and the expense-level `ExpenseProgramAllocationsEditor` ~line 868) showed only `{program.name}`. Programs carry a unique `code` field on the wire (`program.service.ts` list does a full `.select()`); two programs can share a display name while `code` is the authoritative unique key, so allocating grant/expense dollars to the wrong program was a real misposting hazard. FIX: widened both `programs` casts from `{ id; name }` to `{ id; name; code?: string | null }` and render the two-line option (name + muted `{code}` when present, omitted when absent so single-program / code-less orgs are unchanged). (2) EVENT PICKER — the donor volunteer-hours linked-event `<SelectItem>` in `donors/$contactId.tsx` (~line 1168) showed only `{event.name}`. Recurring annual events ("Spring Gala" every year) legitimately share a name; `date` disambiguates. FIX: widened the inline event cast to include `date?: string | null` and render a muted `formatUtcCalendarDate(event.date)` subtitle when present (helper already imported in the file). The `__none__` "No linked event" sentinel left untouched. TDD: grants test statically mocked `usePrograms` with empty data — converted it to a reconfigurable `vi.hoisted` mock (`hoisted.mockUsePrograms`) with the identical empty default restored in `beforeEach` so all prior tests stay green, then added a RED-first test supplying two same-named "Youth Mentoring" programs (codes YM-2025 / YM-2026, `audit_ready` plan tier to enable the allocations editor) asserting both codes render. Donors test added a RED-first test mocking a "Spring Gala" event with date 2026-06-01 asserting "Jun 1, 2026" renders. Both confirmed RED → GREEN (270 passed across the two files). GATE: tsc clean, built web first (build-output dist gotcha), FOREGROUND gated commit ran full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN, per-file coverage held. DELEGATION: implementation+TDD delegated to a sonnet sub-agent in the worktree (orchestrator context preserved); the haiku review sub-agent failed to spawn (system-prompt context overhead exceeded the 200k single-exchange limit), so the small 4-file diff was self-reviewed directly — meaningful non-tautological tests, real import confirmed, empty-string `code` guarded by falsy check, no `any` in prod. Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: eighth/ninth finds; the picker-collision family (Waves 101 reports, 102 payments, 103 fiscal-period, 104 program+event) is now fully worked through per the grep catalogue — only the intentionally-skipped funder/fund `type` pickers (weak disambiguators) remain, documented in Wave 103 as out of scope. General lesson reaffirmed: a record-by-id picker needs a STRONG secondary identity (unique code, authoritative date, funder+period); a weak class/type field is decorative and rightly skipped. Next: move off pickers — sweep events, calendar, dashboard, and deeper data-bound states for the next defect family.

### Wave 105 — Event Revenue Breakdown mislabeled non-donors; blank attendee names (shipped)

- TWO EVENT-DETAIL DEFECTS MERGED (commit de9277ee on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version 4a2e21f2-fdb4-4ed5-94ca-5bd815f949ba). First defect family OFF the picker-collision track — found via a sonnet static audit of the events/calendar/dashboard surfaces (calendar + events-list + new-event dialog returned CLEAN; the audit also flagged a dashboard "Restricted" hardcode + a Today-card UTC issue, queued for a later wave). Both fixes in `routes/_authenticated/events/$eventId.tsx`. (1) REVENUE BREAKDOWN MISLABEL (HIGH) — the Overview "Revenue Breakdown" section did `attendees.map((attendee, index) => <span>{`Donation ${index + 1}`}</span> <span>{formatCurrency(attendee.donation?.amountCents)}</span>)`, i.e. it listed EVERY attendee as "Donation 1", "Donation 2"… including attendees with no linked donation (whose `donation?.amountCents` is undefined → `formatCurrencyCents` "auto" renders `--`/`$0`). An attendee who hasn't given is not a donation; a revenue section padded with non-revenue rows labeled "Donation N" is factually wrong and reads as broken to a paying customer. FIX: derive `donatingAttendees = attendees.filter((a) => a.donation)`, render one row per actual donor labeled by donor NAME (not "Donation N") with the amount, and an empty-state `<p>No donations recorded yet.</p>` when none have given. (2) BLANK ATTENDEE NAME (HIGH/polish) — attendee cards computed `displayName = [firstName, lastName].filter(Boolean).join(" ")`, rendering a completely blank `<div className="font-medium">` when a contact had no first/last name (common for organization contacts). `getEvent` loads attendees `with: { contact: true }`, so `email`/`id` are already on the wire — the local type just narrowed them away. FIX: widened the attendee `contact` type to include `id?`/`email?`, added a shared `attendeeDisplayName(contact)` helper returning `name || email || "Unnamed attendee"`, and used it in BOTH the revenue row and the attendee card (single source of truth). TDD: existing populated-event test asserted the old "Donation 1" label and never checked the amount — rewrote it to assert the donor name + amount and `queryByText("Donation 1")` ABSENT; added 3 new tests (revenue lists only donors / omits non-donors + no `$0` noise; empty-state message when nobody donated; name fallback renders email then "Unnamed attendee"). GATE CAUGHT A SECOND STALE TEST: first full-coverage gated commit FAILED on `src/__tests__/events-pages.test.tsx:451` which also asserted "Donation 1" (its attendee had `donation: null`) — updated it to assert the new "No donations recorded yet." empty state (Sam Rivera still shows as the attendee-card name). CURRENCY-FORMAT GOTCHA: `formatCurrencyCents` defaults to "auto" cents — `25000` renders `$250` (no decimals, integer amount), not `$250.00`; two test assertions corrected, and a summary-card revenue value (`revenueCents`) had to be set distinct from the donation amount to avoid a getByText double-match (the Overview summary card and the revenue row both print the same currency string). Re-ran gate: full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN (4050 tests), per-file coverage held. REVIEW: self-reviewed (sub-agent review unavailable — the worker/system-prompt context overhead exceeds the 200k single-exchange limit for a fresh spawned agent; the diff is small and the new helper/branches are all test-covered). Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: tenth/eleventh finds, both static-detectable (no live session needed) — a "Revenue Breakdown" that iterates ALL rows rather than the rows that actually carry revenue is a logic defect that static review catches once you read the map body; a name built by joining nullable fields without a fallback is the same blank-render hazard as the Wave-99 dangling-badge lesson (prefer a readable fallback over an empty element). QUEUED for next wave (from this audit, verified against source): dashboard.tsx:523 hardcodes "Restricted" under every fund name regardless of actual restriction class (needs API-shape check — widen the overview fundBalances wire to carry restriction, or drop the unsupportable label); dashboard.tsx Today-card formats `new Date()` in UTC which can show tomorrow's weekday for late-evening users in negative-offset timezones. Next: dashboard fund-restriction label + Today-card date, then continue deeper data-bound surfaces.

### Wave 106 — Dashboard fund-restriction label hardcoded + Today-card UTC date drift (shipped)

- TWO DASHBOARD DEFECTS MERGED (commit b43447c3 on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version 36f0c684-d397-4c3a-9b56-bf5c55e55ed5). Acted on the two items Wave 105's audit queued, both verified against source. (1) HARDCODED "Restricted" LABEL (HIGH) — `dashboard.tsx` FundBalancesCard rendered a literal `<p>Restricted</p>` subtitle under EVERY fund name regardless of the fund's actual restriction class. For a compliance product whose whole value is fund-restriction accuracy, mislabeling an unrestricted or permanently-restricted fund as "Restricted" on the primary dashboard is factually wrong and undermines trust. Root cause: the overview wire shape carried the fund `type` on the API side (`DashboardFundBalance.type`, overview/service.ts) but `use-overview.ts` narrowed it away — the mapped `fundBalances` had no fund type at all, so the UI had nothing to render and fell back to a hardcoded string. FIX: threaded `type` through `RawDashboardOverview.fundBalancesOverview` (`type: string`) → mapped `DashboardOverview.fundBalances` gained `fundType: string` (`fundType: fund.type` in the map); dashboard imports `formatFundTypeLabel` and renders `{formatFundTypeLabel(fund.fundType)}` (→ "Unrestricted" / "Temporarily restricted" / "Permanently restricted" via the shared FUND_TYPE_LABELS lookup). (2) TODAY-CARD UTC DATE DRIFT (MEDIUM) — the AgendaView Today card formatted `new Date()` with `timeZone: "UTC"`, so a user in a negative-offset zone during late evening would see TOMORROW's weekday/date in the heading while the rest of the card keys off `todayUtc` (`new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)`) — an internal inconsistency where the headline date and the agenda's notion of "today" disagree. FIX: anchored the formatted heading to the same UTC calendar day the card already computes — `.format(new Date(`${todayUtc}T12:00:00Z`))` (noon-UTC anchor keeps the UTC formatter on the intended calendar day). TDD: strengthened the existing fund-balances render test to supply real `fundType`s (temporarily_restricted + unrestricted) and assert the human labels render AND that the old literal `"Restricted"` is ABSENT (`queryByText("Restricted", { exact: true })` → not present) — this assertion was RED against the hardcoded source and GREEN after; added `fundType`/`type` to every non-empty fund mock in dashboard.test.tsx and use-overview.test.ts plus the two mapped-output expectations. GATE: built web first (build-output.test gotcha), full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN (per-file coverage held — the change added no new branches, only a lookup call and a Date arg), tsc clean. REVIEW: self-reviewed (sub-agent review unavailable — fresh-agent context overhead exceeds the 200k single-exchange cap; diff is 4 files / +70-12, all paths test-covered, and `todayUtc` confirmed in AgendaView scope at line 998 before its use at 1167). Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: both static-detectable — a hardcoded label adjacent to per-row data is always suspect (read whether the row object actually carries the thing the label claims); a `new Date()` formatted in a fixed non-local timezone next to an ISO-slice "today" derived the same way is a self-inconsistency worth catching. The Wave-105 audit's queued items are now both cleared. Next: continue deeper data-bound surfaces — accounting, cash, reports, settings, import, notifications, and the award-intake commit path. Funder/fund weak-`type` pickers remain intentionally skipped.

### Wave 107 — Accounting date inputs defaulted to UTC "today" instead of local (shipped)

- DATE-INPUT DEFAULT DRIFT MERGED (commit 9bfe3fbc on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version 4dfaee98-f570-49b4-b724-fa89692d556d). Found via a sonnet static audit of the accounting + cash surfaces (audit ran the prior waves' detector list: hardcoded labels, raw-cents, blank nullable names, filtered-section mislabels, picker collisions, UTC date drift, dangling badges). The accounting/cash money rendering, badges, name fallbacks, and account pickers (`{code}: {name}`) all came back CLEAN; the one verified defect was a UTC-vs-local "today" drift in user-facing date INPUTS. DEFECT (MEDIUM, family 6): the new-journal-entry date field (`accounting/journal/new.tsx:80`) and the recurring-template next-run date (`accounting/recurring.tsx:94` initial + `:119` dialog-open reset) prefilled `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` — the UTC calendar day. A user west of UTC editing in the evening (e.g. US Pacific after ~4–5pm) would see TOMORROW's date already filled into a field they read as "today" and submit — a quiet correctness/polish defect for an accounting product. Distinct from the deliberate UTC convention for report CUTOFF/"as of" boundaries (trial-balance, financial-position, accounting/index `T23:59:59.999Z`), which are intentionally UTC and were left alone; this is specifically about a default a human reads and submits as a calendar date in their own frame. The edit-mode recurring path (`new Date(editTemplate.nextRunDate).toISOString().slice(0,10)`) reads back a stored UTC calendar date and was correctly left as-is. FIX: added a shared, pure, testable `todayLocalDateInput(now = new Date())` helper to `lib/format.ts` that builds `YYYY-MM-DD` from LOCAL `getFullYear/getMonth/getDate` (matching the existing local-date pattern already inlined in `calendar.tsx:34`), and used it for the three user-facing "today" defaults. TDD: added two RED-first tests (`new Date(2026,0,5,23,30)` → "2026-01-05"; zero-pad `new Date(2026,8,3)` → "2026-09-03") — both constructed in local time so they're deterministic regardless of the runner's TZ; confirmed RED (helper absent → import undefined, 2 failed), implemented helper → GREEN (72 format tests pass). GATE: built web first (build-output.test gotcha), full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN in 3m15s, per-file coverage held (helper fully covered; route files gained no new branches — only the date-init expression changed). tsc clean. REVIEW: self-reviewed (sub-agent review unavailable — fresh-agent context overhead exceeds the 200k single-exchange cap; diff is 4 files / +28-5, the helper is pure and fully tested, the three call sites are mechanical swaps). Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: the UTC-vs-local distinction is now a recognized sub-family of the Wave-106 date-drift find — UTC is right for stored calendar dates and report boundaries, LOCAL is right for a "today" default a human reads and submits; `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` next to a date INPUT default is the smell. Accounting/cash money + badge + picker surfaces audited clean. Next: reports, settings, import, notifications, and the award-intake commit path.

### Wave 108 — Award-intake review showed confidence % for fields the AI never extracted (shipped)

- EMPTY-FIELD CONFIDENCE MISLABEL MERGED (commit feab6cbc on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version dca7d691-f881-409a-9318-247d3cd0b2d9). Found via a sonnet static audit of the award-intake commit path (the AI document-extraction review screen, `components/document-extractions/extraction-review.tsx`); the audit ran the prior waves' detector list plus an intake-specific check (misleading confidence/state indicators). The money rendering and the grant/funder pickers on this surface came back guarded/CLEAN; the one verified defect (family 8, intake-specific): a field the model returned null/blank/empty-object for was still rendered with a "{confidence}% confidence" badge AND an invisible empty `<p>` for its value. For an AI-extraction product whose entire trust proposition is "we tell you how sure we are," pairing a confidence percentage with a value the model never pulled is actively misleading — it implies the AI is 85% sure about something it left blank, and the blank line reads as a rendering bug to a reviewer about to accept the extraction into real grant records. FIX: added a pure `fieldHasValue(field)` helper (null/undefined → false; whitespace-only string → false; empty plain object → false; else true, using the existing `isPlainObject` guard) and threaded it through three render sites — `confidenceLabel` now returns "Not extracted" instead of "{n}% confidence" when the field is empty; `FieldValue` returns an explicit muted `<p>Not extracted from the document</p>` placeholder instead of a blank line; and the badge className treats an empty field as warning-styled (`!fieldHasValue(field) || field.confidence < 70`) so it visually reads as needs-attention rather than a confident muted chip. TDD: extended the RED-first empty-field test to cover all three false-branches of `fieldHasValue` in one render — a null field (confidence 85), a whitespace-only string field (73), and an empty-object field (90) — asserting three "Not extracted" badges, none of the three confidence strings present, and two "Not extracted from the document" placeholders (the empty object is edited through its sub-field inputs, not the scalar placeholder). Confirmed the helper's blank-string and empty-object false-paths were the previously-uncovered branches; removed an unreachable `Array.isArray` branch to keep coverage honest. GATE: built web first (build-output.test dist gotcha), full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN, per-file coverage held (extraction-review.tsx 98.53% stmts / 96.53% branch — above 95%; the only uncovered lines, 191-199, are pre-existing). tsc clean. REVIEW: self-reviewed (sub-agent review unavailable — fresh-agent context overhead exceeds the 200k single-exchange cap; diff is 2 files / +78-1, the helper is pure and fully branch-covered, the three call sites are guard-only). Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: the intake-specific lesson generalizes — any confidence/quality/state indicator must be gated on the underlying datum actually existing; a metric shown next to an absent value is worse than no metric because it manufactures false certainty. Award-intake money + pickers + this confidence-state are now clean. Next: continue the sweep on remaining data-bound surfaces — donors detail, grants detail deeper states, funds, subrecipients/portal, and any not-yet-walked interactive states.

### Wave 109 — Donor communication card showed a blank heading when no subject was entered (shipped)

- BLANK-HEADING FALLBACK MERGED (commit a2275ef3 on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-web version f8c88cba-e041-466d-8c88-1034168173aa). Found via a sonnet static audit of the donor-detail (`donors/$contactId.tsx`) and funds (`funds/index.tsx`, `funds/$fundId.tsx`) surfaces using the prior waves' detector list. Funds list + detail came back CLEAN (all money via formatCurrency, type badge data-driven via `formatFundTypeLabel`, `thresholdState` badge only rendered when non-null, "Grant allocation"/"Expense" are defensive defaults not data-claims). Donor money/name-fallback/event-picker also CLEAN (`formatContactDisplayName` → "Unnamed donor"; giving stats are cents through formatCurrency; event picker already shows date subtitle). The one verified defect (family 7, blank standalone heading): the communications-timeline card rendered `<p className="font-semibold">{comm.subject}</p>` directly, but `subject` is `string | null` — the create validator (`packages/shared/src/validators/donors.ts:175`) requires `subject || body`, so a body-only communication is a NORMAL production state with null subject. That rendered a completely blank bold heading line above the body — reads as a rendering bug to a paying customer reviewing a donor's history. FIX: fall back to the humanized communication type via the file's existing `formatDisplayLabel(comm.type)` helper (→ "Call"/"Email"/"Note"/"Meeting"), using truthiness (`comm.subject || formatDisplayLabel(comm.type)`) not nullish so an empty-string subject (also allowed by the validator when body is present) is covered too. TDD: added a RED-first test adding a sixth mock communication (`type: "call", subject: null, body: "Quick check-in…"`) asserting the heading "Call" renders and the body still shows; confirmed RED (subject null → "Call" absent, 1 failed / 123 passed) then GREEN (124 passed) after the fix. GATE: built web first (build-output dist gotcha), full @grantpipe/web test:coverage GREEN, per-file coverage held ($contactId.tsx 98.89% stmts / 96.13% branch — above 95%). tsc clean. WORKTREE NOTE: a backgrounded gated commit was terminated mid-run (SIGTERM/exit 143 when its wrapping sleep was killed); the staged changes survived, so I re-ran the commit synchronously in the worktree — gate re-ran fully GREEN. REVIEW: self-reviewed (sub-agent review unavailable — fresh-agent context overhead exceeds the 200k single-exchange cap; diff is 2 files / +21-1, a single guard-with-fallback render change plus its test). Merged ff, worktree removed + branch deleted, deployed grantpipe-web via Wrangler. SWEEP NOTE: same blank-render-hazard lesson as Waves 99 (dangling badge) and 105 (nullable-name join) — any nullable field rendered as a standalone heading/badge needs a readable fallback, never an empty element; the tell is a JSX expression printing a `T | null` field directly inside a heading/label element with no `??`/`||`/ternary guard. Donor + funds surfaces now clean. Next: grants-detail deeper interactive states, subrecipients, portal, and any not-yet-walked data-bound states.

### Wave 110 — Grant closeout items showed a raw user UUID instead of the completer's name (shipped)

- CLOSEOUT COMPLETER-NAME JOIN MERGED (commit c1b85e40 on master, ff; deployed grantpipe-api version e8ee4bb0-ea72-4150-bb4f-28aea9b5ea94 + grantpipe-web version ba2d36d0-13e4-429e-9d43-790d36839449). Found via a sonnet static audit of grants-detail (deeper tabs), subrecipients, and the external reviewer portal. Subrecipients list+detail CLEAN; the audit surfaced four candidates, of which this was verified and shipped (the others triaged below). DEFECT (family 3, MEDIUM): the grant closeout-items list (`grants/$grantId.tsx:2714`) rendered `Completed {date}{completedBy ? ` by ${completedBy}` : ""}` where `completedBy` is `grantCloseoutItems.completed_by`, a `text().references(() => user.id)` — i.e. a raw user UUID.
